People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Yeah… kind of. I don’t want to overstate it, because some of it might just be work stress, but there were days where I felt like I couldn’t stop moving around the store — pacing, fixing shelves, straightening things that probably didn’t need straightening.

Maybe a few days here and there where I could kind of pass as normal, I guess… but not really like myself.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve just been really tired. I mean, I’m still getting through work, mostly, but it’s gotten hard to make it through a shift. My body feels kind of heavy, like it’s filled with wet sand, if that makes sense. And resting doesn’t really seem to bring me back the way it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad, exactly. Maybe a little… worn down? I’m trying not to make it sound worse than it is. I still show up and do what I’m supposed to do, but I guess I haven’t felt very bright lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: A little less, I guess. I mean, I still try to do things, but… stuff I usually really like, like cooking for myself or playing guitar, even hiking, it just feels kind of flat right now. Going over to my family’s place too—I’ll do it, but it feels like I’m sort of pretending to be into it. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about anything lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… not especially? I worry about normal stuff, like work and money a bit, but I don’t think it’s been the main thing. I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like I feel drained than panicky or anything. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean, my shifts change around, so I’ve been telling myself it’s probably just that, but there were times I’d only sleep a few hours and still feel kind of wired. And lately, even when I’m really tired, I wake up too early and my mind is already going. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but things I normally really like—cooking, playing guitar, going for hikes, even visiting my family—just kind of feel flat lately. I still do some of them, but it’s like I’m going through the motions or pretending I care more than I actually do. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Um… not like I have a plan or anything. I don’t want you to think I’m being dramatic. But I have had these scary thoughts sometimes, like… maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd a few times, walking home, I caught myself thinking about stepping off the curb into traffic. I didn’t do it, but the thought was there, and it kind of scared me. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe around four months? The low, empty feeling, I mean. It started after that first rush of energy I had at work kind of wore off, and then it just… didn’t really go away. I kept thinking it would pass, so I maybe didn’t take it seriously at first, but yeah, about four months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Um… a little, I think. I haven’t been weighing myself much, so I don’t want to overstate it, but my appetite’s been kind of all over the place. Lately I mostly just forget to eat, or when I do eat, food tastes… like cardboard, I guess. And my work pants have been fitting looser, so I think I’ve probably lost some weight. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think both, actually, at different times. At first I was kind of… busy, I guess. Like I couldn’t stop walking around the store, straightening shelves and fixing things even when they didn’t really need it.\n\nBut lately it’s more the opposite. Even walking to the bus feels like I’m moving through mud, and if someone asks me something, it takes me a second to answer. I don’t mean to be difficult or anything, it just feels… slower and heavy. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: Um… I wasn’t going to mention it because it sounds kind of dramatic, but… yeah, a little.\n\nAt work, at the register, sometimes I’ll hear what sounds like customers right behind me whispering my name, or saying I’m messing everything up. And I’ll turn around expecting someone to be there, but… there isn’t anyone.\n\nI know that probably sounds bad. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is. It’s just happened enough that I noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past month where you felt completely unable to get out of bed or perform daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still try to get up because I don’t want to sound like I’m just not trying, but there have been days where it’s really hard.\n\nHonestly, that’s kind of why I came in. I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift sometimes. It feels like my body is filled with wet sand, and even if I rest, it doesn’t really bring me back. I can usually force myself to do things, but it feels like I’m dragging myself through them. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… maybe a little, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal.\n\nLike, I can still make decisions if I have to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Um… not like I have a plan or anything. I don’t want you to think I’m being dramatic. But I have had these scary thoughts sometimes, like… maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd a few times, walking home, I caught myself thinking about stepping off the curb into traffic. I didn’t do it, but the thought was there, and it kind of scared me. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe around four months? The low, empty feeling, I mean. It started after that first rush of energy I had at work kind of wore off, and then it just… didn’t really go away. I kept thinking it would pass, so I maybe didn’t take it seriously at first, but yeah, about four months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Um… a little, I think. I haven’t been weighing myself much, so I don’t want to overstate it, but my appetite’s been kind of all over the place. Lately I mostly just forget to eat, or when I do eat, food tastes… like cardboard, I guess. And my work pants have been fitting looser, so I think I’ve probably lost some weight. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think both, actually, at different times. At first I was kind of… busy, I guess. Like I couldn’t stop walking around the store, straightening shelves and fixing things even when they didn’t really need it.\n\nBut lately it’s more the opposite. Even walking to the bus feels like I’m moving through mud, and if someone asks me something, it takes me a second to answer. I don’t mean to be difficult or anything, it just feels… slower and heavy. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: Um… I wasn’t going to mention it because it sounds kind of dramatic, but… yeah, a little.\n\nAt work, at the register, sometimes I’ll hear what sounds like customers right behind me whispering my name, or saying I’m messing everything up. And I’ll turn around expecting someone to be there, but… there isn’t anyone.\n\nI know that probably sounds bad. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is. It’s just happened enough that I noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past month where you felt completely unable to get out of bed or perform daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still try to get up because I don’t want to sound like I’m just not trying, but there have been days where it’s really hard.\n\nHonestly, that’s kind of why I came in. I’m so tired I can barely get through a shift sometimes. It feels like my body is filled with wet sand, and even if I rest, it doesn’t really bring me back. I can usually force myself to do things, but it feels like I’m dragging myself through them. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… maybe a little, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal.\n\nLike, I can still make decisions if I have to. Sometimes I’ll just stand there longer than I should over something small, like what to do first or what to buy, and then I feel kind of silly about it. But I wouldn’t want to make it sound like I can’t decide anything. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more fidgety or restless than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I don’t want to overstate it, because some of it might just be work stress, but there were days where I felt like I couldn’t stop moving around the store — pacing, fixing shelves, straightening things that probably didn’t need straightening.\n\nBut lately it’s almost gone the other way. Like even walking to the bus feels slow and heavy, and if someone asks me a question, it takes me a second to answer. I’m still doing things, just… slower, I guess. Does that count as what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past four months where you felt okay, like your normal self, even if it was just for a few days?\n\nPatient: Maybe a few days here and there where I could kind of pass as normal, I guess… but not really like myself.\n\nThe low, empty feeling has been going on for about four months now. At first, after that rush of energy I had at work passed, I thought I was just tired and I’d bounce back. But it’s sort of stayed. I can still function some days, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I don’t think I’ve had a real stretch where I felt okay-okay. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're moving slower than usual most of the time over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety most of the time over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Mostly at the beginning, I think. I was kind of… buzzing around at work, pacing the store and straightening shelves even when they were already fine.\n\nLately it hasn’t been as much fidgety, though. It’s more like I’m slowed down — walking to the bus feels heavy, and even answering people takes me a second. I still try to tell myself it’ll pass, or that I just need to get through the next day. I’ll do it, or I’ll think I should do it, but it feels kind of flat, like I’m just pretending to care or going through the motions.
